gemini-3.8-flash/06-product-planning/RESPONSE.md

Summary of the Three Riskiest Assumptions

Based on the requirements in BRIEF.md and the implementation architecture in PLAN.md, the three riskiest assumptions for adding offline capabilities to the building inspection app are summarized below:

1. Assumption: Intermittent Connectivity Windows are Sufficient for 40-Photo Uploads Without Severe Battery Drain or Operational Delays

  • The Assumption: Field inspectors working in subterranean basements will reliably encounter sufficient cellular or Wi-Fi connectivity windows (e.g., stairwells, vehicle transit, depot return) to transfer up to 40 photos per inspection without manual intervention or system failure.
  • Why It Is Risky: Modern tablet cameras produce high-resolution images (5–15 MB raw each). Even when compressed on-device to ~600 KB, 40 photos represent ~25–30 MB of binary data per inspection. Cellular connectivity at building perimeters or in moving vehicles is notoriously flaky (high packet loss, 2G/EDGE speeds, frequent radio dropouts). If the synchronization engine attempts continuous uploads over degraded links, the cellular radio will remain at maximum power, rapidly draining tablet batteries before a shift ends. Furthermore, stalled uploads delay supervisor review, creating operational bottlenecks.
  • Mitigation Strategy in Plan:
    1. Decoupled two-tier sync: form metadata syncs immediately in seconds (<50 KB), while media syncs asynchronously in the background.
    2. Aggressive on-device image optimization (downscaling to max 2048px, 80% JPEG compression, metadata stripping).
    3. Resumable, chunked multipart uploads with SHA-256 deduplication.
    4. Concurrency throttling (maximum 2 parallel streams) and exponential backoff with jitter to protect battery life and radio health.

2. Assumption: Concurrent Supervisor Web Edits and Offline Field Work Can Be Harmonized Without a Collaborative Editor or Unacceptable Data Loss

  • The Assumption: Supervisors editing inspections via the web portal and inspectors editing offline in basements can coexist using existing integer REST versioning and automated merging, without requiring a full real-time collaborative editor (CRDTs/OT) and without causing destructive data conflicts.
  • Why It Is Risky: Product explicitly rejected a full collaborative editor, yet business operations permit supervisors to edit the same inspection from the web. If an inspector enters a basement with integer record version 3, and a supervisor modifies the inspection on the web (advancing the server to version 4), an optimistic concurrency clash is guaranteed when the inspector reconnects. If conflict resolution is too aggressive (Last-Write-Wins), critical field observations or supervisor compliance edits will be silently destroyed, creating legal and regulatory liability. Conversely, if the server strictly rejects conflicted submissions (HTTP 409), field inspectors will be blocked from submitting completed work.
  • Mitigation Strategy in Plan:
    1. Advisory/soft checkout locking on the web portal: displaying a clear warning to supervisors when an inspection is checked out offline by an inspector in the field.
    2. Server-side three-way field-level merge algorithm for disjoint field edits.
    3. Clear domain authority rules: on-site inspector observations take precedence for physical checklist items and photos, while supervisor edits to non-overlapping administrative fields are preserved.
    4. Non-destructive conflict handling: conflicting supervisor edits are staged in an audit table and flagged with a web notification for supervisor review rather than dropped.

3. Assumption: The 24-Hour Regulatory Customer Data Purge Can Be Reliably Enforced and Forensically Verified on Disconnected Mobile Hardware

  • The Assumption: Client-side background execution and device lifecycle states will reliably execute and verify the complete destruction of local customer PII, signatures, and photos within strictly 24 hours of server acceptance.
  • Why It Is Risky: Mobile tablets are semi-connected, client-controlled edge devices subject to OS constraints outside the application's direct control. Tablets may be turned off at the end of a shift, stored in vehicles or metal lockers over weekends without cellular service, or subjected to aggressive OS battery-saving mechanisms (such as Android Doze or iOS background task throttling) that terminate background workers. If the 24-hour regulatory timer expires while the tablet is offline, powered down, or suspended, sensitive customer data remains on the device, exposing the business to severe regulatory penalties. Furthermore, standard filesystem unlinks do not overwrite flash memory, leaving data potentially recoverable by forensic tools.
  • Mitigation Strategy in Plan:
    1. Dual-control failsafe timer: the server passes accepted_at and purge_deadline_at during synchronization; the tablet enforces this deadline locally, even if completely offline when the deadline arrives.
    2. Boot/launch-level guard: a startup interceptor checks purge deadlines before permitting any user interaction, ensuring that sleeping or newly powered-on devices immediately purge expired data.
    3. Cryptographic shredding: overwriting image and signature files with random bytes prior to unlinking, scrubbing SQLite PII fields, and executing incremental vacuuming.
    4. Verifiable audit receipts: generating a cryptographically signed purge receipt that is queued in the mutation table and transmitted to the server as proof of compliance upon reconnection.

gemini-3.8-flash/06-product-planning/BRIEF.md

Offline inspection brief

Inspectors use company-managed tablets in basements with no signal. Today, a lost connection after opening an inspection can discard notes. They need to open assigned inspections, fill forms, take up to 40 photos, and collect a signature offline. Connectivity may return intermittently. Supervisors can edit the same inspection from the web. Regulations require an audit history and removal of local customer data within 24 hours after an inspection is accepted. Existing server APIs use REST with integer record versions. The mobile app has SQLite but currently treats it as a read-through cache. Product wants a pilot before broad rollout and does not want a full collaborative editor.

gemini-3.8-flash/06-product-planning/PLAN.md

Engineering & Product Plan: Offline Inspection Sync Engine

1. Executive Summary & Product Alignment

This document outlines the architectural, product, and operational plan for introducing offline capabilities to the tablet-based building inspection application. Based strictly on the requirements in BRIEF.md, field building inspectors frequently operate in zero-connectivity basement environments using company-managed tablets. Currently, intermittent or severed network connectivity causes data loss.

This plan transforms the tablet application from an online-dependent app with a read-through SQLite cache into an offline-first, resilient inspection engine. It establishes reliable local data capture for complex forms, up to 40 photos, and legally binding customer signatures, coupled with an asynchronous, conflict-resilient synchronization protocol that integrates with existing integer-versioned REST APIs. It enforces regulatory compliance by guaranteeing cryptographically verifiable local customer data destruction within 24 hours of inspection acceptance.

2. Explicit Assumptions

To ensure complete clarity, the following assumptions are established from BRIEF.md and industry best practices:

  1. Hardware & OS Fleet: Tablets are company-managed devices (iOS or Android) enrolled in an Enterprise Mobility Management / Mobile Device Management (MDM) solution, allowing security policy enforcement (passcodes, full-disk encryption, OS updates).
  2. Assignment Model: Inspections are dispatched and pre-assigned to a single primary inspector at a time. Multi-inspector simultaneous offline editing of the exact same inspection on multiple tablets is not supported.
  3. Web Supervisor Workflow: Supervisors review and occasionally edit inspections via a web portal. Because Product has ruled out a real-time collaborative editor (CRDTs/OT), concurrent edits are handled via coarse-grained optimistic locking and field-aware merging.
  4. Basement Connectivity Profile: Field inspectors experience binary or severely degraded connectivity: completely severed signal while underground, followed by brief, intermittent connectivity windows (e.g., ground floor stairwells, street level, vehicle transit, depot Wi-Fi).
  5. Inspection Lifecycle States: Assigned $\rightarrow$ Downloaded / Local Ready $\rightarrow$ In Progress (Field) $\rightarrow$ Completed & Signed $\rightarrow$ Synced $\rightarrow$ Accepted (Supervisor) $\rightarrow$ Purged (Local Data Scrubbed).
  6. Regulatory Purge Mandate: "Accepted" is an explicit terminal state granted on the server. The 24-hour countdown begins the moment the server marks the inspection as Accepted. All local personally identifiable information (PII), customer signatures, and photos must be purged from tablet storage within 24 hours of this event, even if the tablet is offline when the timer expires.
  7. Server Architecture: The backend uses RESTful endpoints with monotonic integer record versions (version: 1, 2, 3...) for concurrency control.
  8. Attachment Volume: An inspection contains up to 40 photos (uncooked device photos: 5–15 MB each; total raw 200–600 MB) plus 1 vector/raster customer signature (<100 KB). Bandwidth constraints require aggressive on-device compression prior to transmission.

3. Scope Boundaries

In-Scope

  • Offline-First Data Capture: Opening assigned inspections, navigating complex multi-section forms, validating entries, capturing form responses, attaching up to 40 photos, and capturing customer signatures without any network dependency.
  • Local Relational Persistence: Upgrading the local SQLite database from a read-through cache to a fully normalized, local source-of-truth transactional database with Write-Ahead Logging (WAL).
  • Two-Tier Sync Protocol: Decoupling lightweight JSON metadata/form sync from heavy binary attachment sync; background mutation queue with exponential backoff and jitter.
  • Optimistic Version-Aware Conflict Management: Integration with existing integer record versioning; field-level three-way merge on the server for non-overlapping changes; supervisor review flagging for overlapping edits.
  • On-Device Media Pipeline: Background image thumbnailing, resizing, EXIF sanitization, compression, and resumable multipart/chunked upload.
  • Regulatory Compliance & Purge Engine: Tamper-resistant audit logging; automated local customer data shredding within 24 hours of server acceptance with verifiable server receipt.
  • Security at Rest: Application-layer encryption (SQLCipher) and encrypted sandboxed filesystem storage.
  • Phased Pilot & Rollback: Feature-flagged deployment starting with a pilot cohort, supported by non-destructive rollback capabilities.

Out-of-Scope

  • Real-Time Collaborative Editing: Operational Transformation (OT) or Conflict-Free Replicated Data Types (CRDTs) for simultaneous, live collaborative typing between supervisors and inspectors (explicitly excluded by Product).
  • Ad-Hoc Offline Inspection Creation: Creating brand new, unassigned building inspection records while offline without prior server assignment.
  • Peer-to-Peer Tablet Sync: Device-to-device Bluetooth/Wi-Fi Direct syncing between inspectors.
  • Server Architecture Replacement: Migrating backend databases from REST/integer versions to GraphQL, CouchDB, or Firebase.

4. User Flow & Journey

[ Pre-Shift / Depot (Online) ]
  1. Inspector logs in; App authenticates and auto-prefetches assigned inspections.
  2. SQLite stores form templates, historical data, and customer info.
  3. Badge displays: "Downloaded & Ready for Offline".

[ On-Site Field Work (Offline / Basement) ]
  4. Inspector enters basement (Signal Lost -> UI shows "Offline Mode").
  5. Inspector opens inspection instantly (0ms latency, zero blocking spinners).
  6. Inspector fills fields: Auto-saved to SQLite on field blur / step change.
  7. Inspector captures photos (up to 40): Compressed in background, thumbnailed.
  8. Inspector captures customer signature: Vector & bitmap saved with timestamp.
  9. Inspector marks inspection "Completed" (Local validation enforced).

[ Transit / Intermittent Signal Re-acquired ]
  10. OS/Network listener detects connection.
  11. Sync Worker Phase 1: Uploads JSON form mutations & audit log (Lightweight, ~10-50 KB).
  12. Sync Worker Phase 2: Uploads photos sequentially/chunked with resume support.
  13. UI updates badge: "Syncing (12/40 photos)" -> "Fully Synced".

[ Post-Inspection / Supervisor Acceptance (Web & Backend) ]
  14. Supervisor reviews inspection on Web Portal and clicks "Accept Inspection".
  15. Server marks inspection `Accepted` with `accepted_at` timestamp.

[ Regulatory Purge Window (<24 Hours) ]
  16. Next tablet sync receives `Accepted` status and `purge_deadline = accepted_at + 24h`.
  17. At deadline (or immediately upon confirmation if configured):
      - Local customer PII fields in SQLite are scrubbed.
      - Photo and signature binaries in device storage are crypto-shredded.
      - Verifiable audit tombstone sent to server: "Local data purged at [Timestamp]".

5. Pre-Implementation Decisions & Recommendations

Decision Topic Options Evaluated Trade-Offs & Rationale Final Recommendation
1. Conflict Resolution Policy A. Last-Write-Wins (Record Level)
B. Strict Rejection (HTTP 409)
C. Server-Side Field-Level 3-Way Merge		 Option A causes silent data loss. Option B blocks field inspectors from submitting. Option C reconciles non-overlapping fields automatically using integer versions and base versions, flagging true field overlaps for supervisor review. Option C: Server-Side Field-Level 3-Way Merge with Inspector Field Authority for on-site checklist data.
2. Inspection Web Locking A. Hard Pessimistic Lock
B. No Locking
C. Soft / Advisory Lock on Web		 Option A prevents supervisors from making urgent corrections if an inspector is delayed. Option B maximizes collision risk. Option C warns supervisors with an active banner: "Inspector is currently offline on-site. Concurrent edits may create conflicts." Option C: Soft Advisory Lock on Web Portal with prominent banner and explicit confirmation modal before editing.
3. Local Database & File Encryption A. OS Standard Sandbox Only
B. SQLCipher + OS Encrypted File API		 Option A relies solely on tablet MDM passcodes; vulnerable if device is compromised or backup extracted. Option B ensures regulatory-grade encryption at rest using hardware Keystore/Keychain-backed keys. Option B: SQLCipher + EncryptedFile API with AES-256 keys anchored in Android Keystore / iOS Keychain.
4. Photo Upload Architecture A. Base64 inside JSON sync
B. Monolithic REST multipart
C. Dedicated Resumable Object Storage (S3/GCS)		 Option A blows up JSON payloads and fails on flaky links. Option B re-starts entire batch on connection drop. Option C enables independent, parallel, resumable chunked uploads per image. Option C: Presigned Direct-to-Storage Resumable Uploads coordinated via REST attachment metadata endpoints.
5. 24-Hour Purge Enforcement A. Client-only timer
B. Server push notification only
C. Dual-Control Fail-Safe Timer		 Option A risks clock tampering. Option B fails if tablet remains offline after job completion. Option C pairs server-issued UTC timestamp with local monotonic clock failsafe that triggers regardless of network state. Option C: Dual-Control Fail-Safe Timer with local background worker and cryptographic deletion confirmation receipt.

6. Local Data Model (SQLite Architecture)

The tablet SQLite database transitions from a volatile read-through cache to an ACID-compliant, offline-first transactional store.

6.1 Database Pragmas & Configuration

PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;
PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;          -- Write-Ahead Logging for concurrent read/write
PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL;        -- Optimal durability and write performance
PRAGMA auto_vacuum = INCREMENTAL;   -- Reclaims space after photo/data purge

6.2 Schema Definition

-- 1. Inspections Table
CREATE TABLE inspections (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                       -- UUID v4
    server_id INTEGER UNIQUE,                  -- Backend primary key (nullable before first sync)
    template_id TEXT NOT NULL,                 -- Form template identifier
    assigned_inspector_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,                      -- 'assigned', 'in_progress', 'completed', 'synced', 'accepted', 'purged'
    server_version INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1, -- Integer record version from server
    base_version INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,   -- Version tablet branched from
    customer_name TEXT,                        -- PII (Subject to 24h purge)
    customer_address TEXT,                     -- PII (Subject to 24h purge)
    customer_phone TEXT,                       -- PII (Subject to 24h purge)
    inspection_data_json TEXT NOT NULL,        -- Structured form answers/responses
    created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,               -- UTC epoch milliseconds
    updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    accepted_at INTEGER,                       -- Server acceptance timestamp (starts 24h clock)
    purge_deadline_at INTEGER,                 -- accepted_at + 86,400,000 ms
    sync_status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'clean'  -- 'clean', 'dirty', 'syncing', 'conflict', 'error'
);

-- 2. Attachments Table (Photos and Signatures)
CREATE TABLE attachments (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                       -- UUID v4
    inspection_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    attachment_type TEXT NOT NULL,             -- 'photo', 'signature'
    caption TEXT,
    local_file_path TEXT NOT NULL,             -- Sandboxed private storage path
    thumbnail_path TEXT,
    file_size_bytes INTEGER NOT NULL,
    sha256_hash TEXT NOT NULL,                 -- For idempotency & integrity check
    mime_type TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'image/jpeg',
    upload_status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending', -- 'pending', 'uploading', 'uploaded', 'failed'
    remote_url TEXT,
    uploaded_bytes INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
    created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    purged_at INTEGER,
    FOREIGN KEY (inspection_id) REFERENCES inspections(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
);

-- 3. Mutation Queue (Outbox Pattern)
CREATE TABLE mutation_queue (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                       -- UUID v4
    inspection_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    mutation_type TEXT NOT NULL,               -- 'UPDATE_FORM', 'SUBMIT_INSPECTION', 'ATTACH_FILE'
    payload_json TEXT NOT NULL,
    base_version INTEGER NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'queued',     -- 'queued', 'in_flight', 'failed'
    retry_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    last_error TEXT,
    created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (inspection_id) REFERENCES inspections(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
);

-- 4. Immutable Local Audit Log
CREATE TABLE local_audit_log (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                       -- UUID v4
    inspection_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    user_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    action TEXT NOT NULL,                      -- 'FIELD_EDIT', 'PHOTO_CAPTURED', 'SIGNATURE_CAPTURED', 'INSPECTION_SUBMITTED', 'PURGE_EXECUTED'
    field_key TEXT,
    old_value TEXT,
    new_value TEXT,
    timestamp INTEGER NOT NULL,
    device_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    synced_to_server INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, -- Boolean: 0 = pending, 1 = synced
    FOREIGN KEY (inspection_id) REFERENCES inspections(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
);

-- Indexes for performance
CREATE INDEX idx_inspections_sync_status ON inspections(sync_status);
CREATE INDEX idx_inspections_purge_deadline ON inspections(purge_deadline_at);
CREATE INDEX idx_attachments_upload ON attachments(upload_status);
CREATE INDEX idx_mutation_queue_status ON mutation_queue(status, created_at);
CREATE INDEX idx_audit_log_synced ON local_audit_log(synced_to_server);

7. Sync Protocol & Network Resilience

7.1 Architecture: Decoupled Two-Tier Synchronization

Because cellular data in basements or stairwells is intermittent, the sync engine decouples lightweight form updates from heavy media assets.

       [ Tablet Client ]                                     [ REST Backend ]
              |                                                     |
              | ====== TIER 1: Form & Metadata (JSON) ======        |
              | POST /api/v1/inspections/{id}/sync                  |
              | (Payload: changes, base_version: 3, audit_events)   |
              | --------------------------------------------------> |
              |                                                     | Inspects version:
              |                                                     | If server_version == 3:
              |                                                     |   Applies diff, version -> 4
              | <-------------------------------------------------- |
              | HTTP 200 OK { new_version: 4, status: "merged" }    |
              |                                                     |
              | ====== TIER 2: Attachments (Resumable) ======       |
              | POST /api/v1/inspections/{id}/attachments/init      |
              | (Payload: id, sha256, size, type: "photo")          |
              | --------------------------------------------------> |
              | <-------------------------------------------------- |
              | HTTP 200 OK { upload_url: "https://...", token }    |
              |                                                     |
              | PUT [upload_url] (Chunked / Resumable Binary)       |
              | --------------------------------------------------> |
              | <-------------------------------------------------- |
              | HTTP 200 OK (ETag / Checksum confirmed)             |
              |                                                     |
              | POST /api/v1/inspections/{id}/attachments/confirm   |
              | --------------------------------------------------> |
              | <-------------------------------------------------- |

7.2 Idempotency & Batching

  • Every mutation in the mutation_queue carries an immutable client-generated UUID (mutation_id).
  • The server maintains an idempotency_keys table. Re-submitting the same mutation due to network dropouts returns the previous successful HTTP response without duplicating side effects.
  • Mutations for the same inspection are squashed locally before transmission: multiple updates to the same form field within the offline session are collapsed to the final state while preserving all intermediate changes in the local_audit_log.

7.3 Intermittent Network Handling

  • Network detection uses OS connectivity callbacks (ConnectivityManager.NetworkCallback on Android, NWPathMonitor on iOS).
  • When a transition to CONNECTED occurs, the app does not blast the server immediately. It applies an initial stabilization delay (2 seconds) followed by exponential backoff with jitter: $$\text{Delay} = \min(60\text{s}, 2^{\text{retry_count}} \times 1\text{s}) \pm \text{jitter}(0-500\text{ms})$$
  • Background jobs use native OS background job schedulers (WorkManager with NetworkType.CONNECTED constraints on Android, BGAppRefreshTask / BGProcessingTask on iOS).

8. Conflict Policy & Concurrent Web Edits

8.1 The Concurrency Challenge

The server utilizes integer record versions (1, 2, 3...). A conflict occurs when:

  1. Tablet checks out inspection at version $V_{\text{base}} = 3$.
  2. Inspector goes offline in a basement.
  3. Supervisor edits the inspection via the web portal $\rightarrow$ Server advances version to $V_{\text{server}} = 4$.
  4. Inspector comes online and attempts to sync with $V_{\text{base}} = 3$.

8.2 Three-Way Field-Level Merge Algorithm

When the server receives a sync request where $V_{\text{base}} \neq V_{\text{server}}$:

  1. Change Set Extraction: The server loads the audit trail of modifications between $V_{\text{base}}$ and $V_{\text{server}}$ to identify which exact fields the supervisor modified: $\Delta_{\text{supervisor}}$.
  2. Disjoint Field Set (Automatic Merge):
    • If $\Delta_{\text{inspector}} \cap \Delta_{\text{supervisor}} = \emptyset$: The inspector and supervisor edited completely different fields (e.g., supervisor updated customer contact phone, inspector completed basement foundation checklist).
    • Resolution: The server applies both change sets, increments version to $V_{\text{server}} + 1$, persists all audit events, and returns HTTP 200 with the updated full state.
  3. Overlapping Field Set (Domain-Specific Arbitration):
    • If $\Delta_{\text{inspector}} \cap \Delta_{\text{supervisor}} \neq \emptyset$: Both parties modified the same form field (e.g., "Foundation Structural Rating").
    • Rule 1: On-Site Physical Precedence: For field observations, photos, and checklist items, the on-site inspector's observation takes immediate precedence over desk-based edits, as the inspector is physically witnessing the building condition.
    • Rule 2: Supervisor Review Flag: Rather than rejecting the submission or silently overwriting, the server accepts the inspector's value as primary, writes the supervisor's prior edit to a conflict_history log, and triggers a web notification to the supervisor:

      "Field Conflict Notice: Inspector submitted on-site values for 'Foundation Structural Rating' which superseded your web edit made at 14:15. Please review the audit comparison."

    • Rule 3: Status Immutability: If the supervisor marked the inspection as Cancelled or Reassigned, the tablet sync is halted, changes are safely preserved in a server review stash, and the inspector is notified.

9. Attachments Pipeline (Photos & Signatures)

9.1 The Photo Volume Problem

  • Requirement: Up to 40 photos per inspection.
  • Modern tablet cameras produce 12 MP images (~5–10 MB JPEG) or 48 MP RAW images. Uploading $40 \times 8\text{ MB} = 320\text{ MB}$ over poor 3G/intermittent cellular connections will cause timeouts, severe battery drain, and failed syncs.

9.2 On-Device Media Processing Pipeline

  1. Capture: Photo taken via in-app camera interface. Raw bitmap saved temporarily in app cache.
  2. Compression Worker (Executed on background thread immediately after capture):
    • Downscale maximum dimension to 2048px (preserving aspect ratio).
    • Encode as JPEG at 80% quality factor.
    • Strip unnecessary EXIF metadata (GPS/PII stripped or normalized into structured metadata table).
    • Result: File size drops from ~8 MB to 500–800 KB. Total 40-photo payload drops from ~320 MB to ~25 MB.
  3. Thumbnail Generation: Generate a $256 \times 256$ WebP thumbnail (~15 KB) stored locally for instantaneous tablet gallery rendering without loading full bitmaps.
  4. Integrity & Deduplication: Compute SHA-256 hash of the compressed file. Store in attachments table.
[ Camera Capture ] 
       │
       ▼
[ Background Compression Worker ]
  ├── Resize (Max 2048px)
  ├── 80% JPEG Compression (~600 KB)
  ├── Strip PII EXIF
  ├── Generate 256px WebP Thumbnail
  └── Compute SHA-256 Hash
       │
       ▼
[ Encrypted Sandbox Storage ] ──> Record in SQLite (Status: 'pending')

9.3 Resumable Upload Queue

  • Attachments are uploaded independently from form data.
  • WorkManager / Background URLSession dispatches uploads with a concurrency limit of 2 parallel streams to prevent starving cellular radio bandwidth.
  • Each photo upload is chunked (or uses presigned S3/GCS multipart with HTTP Content-Range).
  • If signal drops on photo #18, bytes uploaded for photo #18 resume from the last committed chunk, while photos #1 through #17 remain completed.

9.4 Signature Capture Pipeline

  • Captured via HTML5 Canvas / Native Touch View.
  • Stored as:
    1. Scaled PNG bitmap with transparent background (<50 KB).
    2. SVG vector path data + timestamped cryptographic envelope.
  • Checksum is calculated over: SHA256(customer_signature_bytes + customer_name + timestamp + inspection_id).
  • This ensures tamper evidence: any post-signature alteration of the inspection data invalidates the signature envelope.

10. Security Architecture & Regulatory Compliance

10.1 Storage Security at Rest

  1. Database Encryption: SQLite database is fully encrypted using SQLCipher (AES-256 in CBC mode with HMAC-SHA512 page integrity). The encryption key is dynamically generated and stored in the hardware-backed keystore (Android Keystore / iOS Keychain) under biometric or device-passcode authentication.
  2. Filesystem Sandbox: All photo and signature files are stored in the application's private sandbox directory (no-backup directory to prevent unencrypted cloud backups to iCloud or Google Drive).
  3. Transport Security: All API traffic strictly enforced via TLS 1.3 with Certificate Pinning.

10.2 Tamper-Resistant Regulatory Audit Trail

  • Regulations require an immutable audit trail of every inspection action.
  • The local_audit_log records every field entry, modification, deletion, camera capture, and sync event with: { id, inspection_id, user_id, action, field_key, old_val, new_val, timestamp, device_id }.
  • Audit log records are append-only. SQLite triggers prevent UPDATE or DELETE on the local_audit_log table.
  • Audit events are streamed to the server during every sync pulse.

10.3 The 24-Hour Regulatory Customer Data Purge Engine

Requirement: "Removal of local customer data within 24 hours after an inspection is accepted."

[ Server Marks 'Accepted' ] 
       │
       ▼
[ Sync Notification to Tablet ] ──> Stores accepted_at & purge_deadline_at
       │
       ▼
[ Purge Trigger: Reaching Deadline OR Explicit Server Purge Command ]
       │
       ├── 1. Crypto-Shredding Filesystem Binaries:
       │      Overwrite file bytes with zeros/random bytes, then unlink (Photos & Signatures).
       │
       ├── 2. Database Data Scrubbing:
       │      UPDATE inspections SET customer_name=NULL, customer_address=NULL,
       │                             customer_phone=NULL, inspection_data_json='{}',
       │                             status='purged' WHERE id = :id;
       │      DELETE FROM attachments WHERE inspection_id = :id;
       │
       ├── 3. Execute PRAGMA incremental_vacuum;
       │
       ├── 4. Generate Local Audit Log:
       │      Log ACTION: 'REGULATORY_PURGE_EXECUTED', timestamp, cert_hash.
       │
       └── 5. Transmit Verification Receipt to Server:
              POST /api/v1/inspections/{id}/purge-receipt

Failsafe Offline Purge

If a tablet is powered off or kept in a disconnected locker for 5 days after an inspection is accepted:

  • The purge_deadline_at timestamp is already burned into SQLite.
  • Upon device boot or app launch, the PurgeEnforcementWorker runs before any user interaction.
  • If CurrentTime >= purge_deadline_at, the purge executes immediately offline.
  • The deletion receipt is queued in the mutation_queue to sync whenever connectivity is re-established.

11. Migration & Backfill Strategy

11.1 Upgrading SQLite from Read-Through Cache to Authoritative Store

Currently, the mobile app treats SQLite as an ephemeral read-through cache. Transitioning requires a non-destructive migration path:

  1. Schema Versioning: Use SQLite PRAGMA user_version to manage atomic migrations.
    • Version 0: Legacy read-through cache tables.
    • Version 1: Add mutation_queue, local_audit_log, attachments, and update inspections with version tracking columns (server_version, base_version, sync_status).
  2. Safe Migration Sequence:
    • On app update launch, check if legacy cache contains un-synced dirty records (unlikely in pure read-through, but defensively checked).
    • Execute migration script inside a single SQLite transaction.
    • Flush volatile cache tables; re-initialize schema.
  3. Data Backfill & Pre-Warm:
    • App invokes new server endpoint: GET /api/v1/inspections/assigned-package.
    • Server returns complete offline payloads for all inspections currently assigned to the logged-in inspector:
      • Form templates and dynamic field validation schemas.
      • Inspection metadata and customer contact records.
      • Historical building notes.
    • Tablet populates SQLite, marks status as clean, and downloads reference assets.

11.2 Backward Compatibility with Existing Server REST APIs

  • Existing server endpoints use integer record versions (PUT /api/v1/inspections/{id}).
  • Rather than rewriting existing endpoints, introduce versioned sync endpoints:
    • POST /api/v1/inspections/{id}/sync (Accepts base version, field delta array, and audit entries).
    • Existing web portal endpoints continue operating; the server's internal service layer delegates both web and mobile edits through the common optimistic locking & version increment logic.

12. Observability & Telemetry Framework

Because offline apps operate in a black box during field work, specialized observability is required to track sync health without violating customer privacy.

12.1 Buffered Local Telemetry

  • Tablet logs structured metrics to a lightweight local buffer table client_metrics:
    • offline_duration_ms: Time elapsed between disconnection and re-connection.
    • form_save_latency_ms: SQLite transaction write latency.
    • sync_queue_depth: Number of pending mutations awaiting upload.
    • upload_retry_count: Number of network failures per photo.
    • purge_execution_delay_ms: Difference between purge_deadline_at and actual execution.
  • Metrics are batched and uploaded to the observability backend (POST /api/v1/telemetry) during regular sync windows.

12.2 Critical Server-Side Metrics & Alerts

  • Conflict Rate Metric: $\frac{\text{Syncs with } V_{\text{base}} \neq V_{\text{server}}}{\text{Total Sync Requests}}$. Alert threshold: $> 3%$.
  • Sync Failure Rate: Alert threshold: $> 0.5%$ of mutation submissions failing after 5 retries.
  • Photo Upload Throughput: P50, P90, P99 upload durations per MB.
  • Purge Compliance Monitor: High-priority compliance alert triggered if server has not received a purge-receipt for an accepted inspection within 25 hours (24h SLA + 1h buffer).

13. Testing & Quality Assurance Plan

Test Phase Scope & Scenarios Verification Method
1. Unit & Database Tests • SQLite schema migrations from user_version 0 to 1.
• Idempotent insert/update in mutation_queue.
• Local 3-way merge logic and squashing functions.
• Photo compression algorithms (aspect ratio, quality, EXIF removal).		 Automated CI tests using SQLite in-memory and Android/iOS unit test runners.
2. Network Chaos & Flakiness • Basement Simulation: Sever connection immediately after form edits.
• Intermittent Signal: 10% packet drop, 500kbps bandwidth, 2000ms latency.
• Mid-Flight Disconnect: Sever connection while photo 23 of 40 is 50% uploaded.		 Network Link Conditioner (iOS) & Charles Proxy / Toxiproxy throttling on Android. Verify resumption from photo 23 without re-uploading 1–22.
3. Concurrency & Conflict Simulation • Inspector goes offline with version 4.
• Supervisor modifies field X on web (version 5).
• Inspector modifies field Y offline.
• Both sync: verify clean merge, version 6.
• Test overlapping edit on field Z: verify on-site inspector precedence and supervisor conflict flag.		 Automated API integration tests simulating concurrent client requests against REST backend.
4. Regulatory Purge Verification • Time-travel testing: Advance tablet system clock past 24 hours while offline.
• Verify customer PII fields in SQLite are scrubbed to NULL.
• Forensic filesystem check: Verify photo and signature files cannot be recovered via file carving tools.
• Verify deletion receipt is generated and sent upon reconnect.		 Device test suite with direct SQLite inspection and raw filesystem byte verification.

14. Staged Rollout Strategy

Product explicitly requires a pilot program prior to broad rollout to validate reliability in real-world basement conditions.

┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Stage 0: Internal Dogfooding (QA & Field Champions)        │
│ 5 Devices | Simulated Basements | 100% Synthetic Tests     │
└─────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                              │ Exit Criteria: Zero data loss, 100% purge compliance
                              ▼
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Stage 1: Pilot Program (Single Branch / Depot)             │
│ 15-20 Inspectors | 2 Supervisors | 2-3 Weeks Live Inspections│
│ Feature Flag: `offline_sync_pilot = true`                  │
└─────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                              │ Exit Criteria: Conflict rate < 2%, Zero photo loss,
                              │ P95 sync time < 45s, Inspector NPS positive
                              ▼
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Stage 2: Regional Rollout (25% -> 50% Fleet)               │
│ Feature Flag targeted by Depot ID                          │
└─────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                              │ Exit Criteria: Server error rate < 0.1%,
                              │ DB performance nominal
                              ▼
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Stage 3: Broad Production Rollout (100% Fleet)             │
│ Deprecate legacy read-through cache codebase               │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Pilot Selection & Feedback Loops

  • Pilot Depot: Select a depot operating in high-density urban areas with deep concrete basements and subterranean garages.
  • Instrumentation: Daily automated health summaries comparing field submissions vs web approvals.
  • Weekly Pilot Retrospectives: Direct feedback sessions between engineering, product, and pilot inspectors.

15. Rollback Strategy & Emergency Safeguards

A critical failure during rollout (e.g., local database corruption, sync deadlock) requires an immediate, non-destructive rollback mechanism.

15.1 Emergency Kill Switch

  • Controlled via remote configuration / feature flag service: offline_sync_enabled.
  • When flipped to false:
    1. The tablet app immediately disables the offline background sync engine.
    2. The UI falls back to the legacy online REST mode.

15.2 Safeguard Against Un-synced Field Work (Zero Data Loss Guarantee)

  • Never wipe the local database upon rollback!
  • If the kill switch is triggered while a tablet has un-synced inspections in the local mutation_queue:
    1. The app displays an emergency notification: "System update in progress. You have offline changes stored safely on this tablet."
    2. A dedicated diagnostic screen enables the inspector to trigger an Emergency Data Flush (submitting raw captured JSON directly to an S3 rescue bucket or emergency dump endpoint).
    3. Photos stored locally remain intact in the encrypted sandbox until manual verification confirms receipt.

16. Milestone Sequence, Dependencies & Acceptance Criteria

[ M1: Local Relational Store ] ─────┐
                                    ▼
[ M2: Media & Signature Pipeline ] ───> [ M4: Two-Way Sync Engine ] ───> [ M6: Observability & Flags ]
                                    ▲                                        │
[ M3: Server REST Sync Endpoints ] ─┘                                        ▼
                                                                        [ M7: Depot Pilot ]
[ M5: Security & 24h Purge System ] ─────────────────────────────────────────┤
                                                                             ▼
                                                                        [ M8: Fleet Rollout ]

Milestone 1: Local Architecture & SQLite Engine Refactor

  • Dependencies: None.
  • Key Deliverables:
    • SQLite schema migration from cache to relational model with WAL mode.
    • CRUD operations for forms, inspections, and audit logs.
    • In-memory test suite for offline state management.
  • Acceptance Criteria:
    • App opens, reads, and writes inspection forms with zero network connectivity.
    • App restart during form entry restores 100% of field state.
    • Zero disk leaks or unhandled SQLite lock exceptions.

Milestone 2: On-Device Media & Signature Pipeline

  • Dependencies: Milestone 1.
  • Key Deliverables:
    • Camera integration with background compression worker (max 2048px, 80% JPEG).
    • Thumbnail generation (256px WebP).
    • Touch-based signature capture with SHA-256 cryptographic hashing.
    • Attachment storage table management.
  • Acceptance Criteria:
    • Tablet captures 40 photos without memory pressure or UI frame drops.
    • Total on-disk footprint for 40 photos is $\le 30\text{ MB}$.
    • Signature capture outputs both vector points and PNG with valid checksum.

Milestone 3: Server Sync Endpoints & Conflict Detection Engine

  • Dependencies: Milestone 1.
  • Key Deliverables:
    • Server endpoints: POST /api/v1/inspections/{id}/sync, POST .../attachments/init, POST .../confirm.
    • Integer version validation and 3-way field-level merge algorithm.
    • Web portal advisory lock banner for checked-out inspections.
  • Acceptance Criteria:
    • Server successfully merges disjoint field updates between web supervisor and mobile client.
    • Server rejects or flags overlapping field conflicts without dropping inspector on-site checklist data.
    • Idempotency key prevents duplicate records on re-sent requests.

Milestone 4: Two-Way Sync Protocol & Background Worker

  • Dependencies: Milestone 2, Milestone 3.
  • Key Deliverables:
    • Native background workers (WorkManager / BGTasks) managing mutation_queue.
    • Two-tier sync orchestrator: Metadata sync followed by chunked photo uploads.
    • Exponential backoff with jitter and network listener.
  • Acceptance Criteria:
    • Form notes sync within 5 seconds of reconnecting to cellular network.
    • Interrupted photo uploads resume from last successful chunk.
    • All 40 photos successfully sync over an intermittent 3G simulated link.

Milestone 5: Security Architecture & 24-Hour Purge System

  • Dependencies: Milestone 4.
  • Key Deliverables:
    • SQLCipher database encryption integration with OS Keystore.
    • Private filesystem sandbox protection with encrypted file APIs.
    • 24-hour post-acceptance regulatory purge worker with cryptographic shredding.
    • Server purge receipt verification endpoint.
  • Acceptance Criteria:
    • Local customer PII, photos, and signatures are completely scrubbed within 24 hours of server acceptance timestamp.
    • Purge occurs even if tablet remains offline when deadline expires.
    • Cryptographic purge verification receipt successfully delivered to server.

Milestone 6: Observability, Telemetry & Feature Flagging

  • Dependencies: Milestone 4, Milestone 5.
  • Key Deliverables:
    • Local buffered metrics logging and upload service.
    • Server dashboard for conflict rates, sync latency, and purge compliance.
    • Dynamic feature flags (offline_sync_pilot) with emergency kill switch.
  • Acceptance Criteria:
    • Dashboard displays real-time sync metrics and conflict rates.
    • Flipping feature flag gracefully disables offline sync without deleting un-synced data.

Milestone 7: Field Pilot Deployment

  • Dependencies: Milestone 6.
  • Key Deliverables:
    • Deployment to pilot depot (15–20 inspectors, 2 supervisors).
    • Daily triage and sync telemetry monitoring.
    • Weekly stakeholder reviews.
  • Acceptance Criteria:
    • Zero lost inspections or lost notes over a 2-week active field evaluation period.
    • Conflict rate remains $< 2%$.
    • 100% regulatory purge compliance verified within the 24-hour SLA.

Milestone 8: Full Fleet Production Rollout

  • Dependencies: Milestone 7.
  • Key Deliverables:
    • Phased rollout to 100% of inspector fleet.
    • Removal of legacy read-through cache code paths.
    • Final operational documentation and runbooks.
  • Acceptance Criteria:
    • 100% of company tablets running offline sync engine successfully.
    • Legacy code deprecated and removed.

Task 06 — Product and engineering planning: offline inspection sync

Create PLAN.md for adding offline work to a tablet-based building inspection app. Use BRIEF.md as the only product input.

The plan should make assumptions explicit and cover user flow, scope boundaries, local data model, sync protocol, conflict policy, attachments, security, migration/backfill, observability, staged rollout, rollback, and testing. Include a milestone sequence with dependencies and acceptance criteria. Identify the decisions that must be made before implementation and offer a recommendation for each. Avoid fake dates and unjustified staffing estimates.

Shortest plan. It touches each heading in a few bullets but lacks depth: there is no decision on encryption at rest beyond app-private storage, no decisions milestone, and little on server audit or web write paths. Same-field conflicts resolve server-wins with a toast, which overwrites the inspector's offline notes, the exact failure the brief wants fixed. Purge is scheduled at accepted_at + 24h, and the alert at 20h is after the device should already have acted. The rollout uses invented cohort sizes, percentages and a two-week soak.

Weaknesses

  • Purge at 'accepted_at + 24h' (line 30) with no path for a device that never learns of acceptance

mimo/06-product-planning/RESPONSE.md

RESPONSE — three riskiest assumptions

  1. Offline auth stays usable for a full shift (A1/D5). If tokens die or the server demands fresh auth while the inspector is in a basement, every other offline feature is unreachable. This is the single point of failure before capture even starts. Mitigation: device-bound cached credentials gated by a daily biometric/passcode unlock (D5); validate on real devices in S0 before building anything else.

  2. Conflict policy of "server (supervisor) wins on same-field edit" is acceptable to inspectors and compliance (A8/D4). If field staff cannot accept losing a locally edited value (even with an audit copy and a toast), the product needs merge UI or offline edit leases — a large scope increase the brief explicitly forbids. Mitigation: put the conflict dialog in front of pilot users in S0 and treat any "I lost my notes" complaint as a gate failure, not a support ticket.

  3. The 24h post-acceptance local purge requirement can be met with a purge receipt (hash list) rather than a local audit replica (A4/D6). If regulators require on-device audit history after acceptance, purge and retention collide, the data model changes, and mobile storage may never be demonstrably clean. Mitigation: confirm with compliance before M4; the receipt design is a cheap extension point but the underlying deletion is not reversible, so this must be locked before the first real acceptance in the pilot.

mimo/06-product-planning/BRIEF.md

Offline inspection brief

Inspectors use company-managed tablets in basements with no signal. Today, a lost connection after opening an inspection can discard notes. They need to open assigned inspections, fill forms, take up to 40 photos, and collect a signature offline. Connectivity may return intermittently. Supervisors can edit the same inspection from the web. Regulations require an audit history and removal of local customer data within 24 hours after an inspection is accepted. Existing server APIs use REST with integer record versions. The mobile app has SQLite but currently treats it as a read-through cache. Product wants a pilot before broad rollout and does not want a full collaborative editor.

mimo/06-product-planning/PLAN.md

Plan: Offline work for tablet building-inspection app

1. Assumptions (explicit)

# Assumption If wrong
A1 Company-managed tablets have a local auth session (token or cert) that remains valid for a full offline shift (≥ 24h) Need offline-issued short-lived credentials or a device unlock flow
A2 Assigned inspections can be listed and fully downloaded before signal is lost; inspectors know which assignment they will work Need an always-cached "my queue" and forced prefetch on assignment
A3 Acceptance (supervisor sign-off) is a server-side state transition; the tablet only submits, it does not accept Acceptance path also needs offline rules; scope grows
A4 "Removal of local customer data within 24h after acceptance" means: after sync confirms accepted, the device may purge that inspection; no need to keep a local audit replica Must retain a minimal audit stub locally and prove retention to auditors
A5 Forms are structured (schema-versioned fields + free-text notes), not freeform documents Richer offline editing model required
A6 Photo metadata (time, inspector, inspection id) is the compliance artifact; exact original bytes may be recompressed for transfer Must keep original bytes bit-for-bit
A7 Integer record versions on the server are monotonically increasing per inspection record and can be used as sync tokens Need a richer revision/history API
A8 One inspector owns field edits on a given inspection during a session; supervisor edits are rare and can lose to "last writer with version check" rather than merge True multi-author field-level merge is required (out of scope per product)
A9 Pilot devices can receive app updates and server feature flags; pilot users tolerate occasional re-sync prompts No remote control channel; bake everything into releases

2. Goals / non-goals

Goals

  • Open assigned inspections, fill forms, attach up to 40 photos, capture a signature with zero connectivity.
  • Survive intermittent connectivity with automatic retry; never silently discard user-entered data.
  • Keep a tamper-evident audit history of field-level changes and sync events.
  • Purge customer data from the device within 24h of server-side acceptance.
  • Pilot with a small cohort before broad rollout.

Non-goals

  • Real-time collaborative editing / CRDTs / presence.
  • Offline creation of brand-new inspections.
  • Offline editing by supervisors on the web (web stays online-only).
  • Cross-device handoff of an in-progress offline inspection.

3. User flow

  1. Prep (online): Inspector signs in, opens My assignments. App downloads each assigned inspection (schema + current answers + photo list + version) and marks it local-ready. Badge: "Available offline".
  2. Site (offline): Inspector opens a local-ready inspection. Forms save to SQLite on every field commit. Photos write to app-private storage with a local blob id; thumbnails cached. Signature captured as vector + raster; hash recorded.
  3. Intermittent sync: When the network returns (app foreground, or background fetch every N minutes when permitted), the app drains a per-inspection outbox: metadata/answers first, then attachments in priority order (signature > small fields > photos). Progress UI shows pending count. Retry with exponential backoff + jitter; never drop an outbox entry without either success or explicit user confirmation of abandon.
  4. Submit offline: Inspector marks the inspection ready-to-submit. If offline, it queues as the final outbox item. On successful submit, server returns new version + submitted state.
  5. Acceptance (supervisor, web): Supervisor reviews, may edit (online). Server bumps version and records audit. When state becomes accepted, the next device sync response includes accepted + purge-after timestamp (accepted_at + 24h).
  6. Purge: App deletes local answers, photos, signature, and derived thumbnails for that inspection at purge time (or on next launch if device was off), keeps only a purge receipt (inspection id, purge time, content hashes) unless A4 says otherwise, and reports purge to the server for compliance.

4. Scope boundaries

  • Offline is available only for inspections that were successfully opened/downloaded while online at least once ("local-ready set").
  • Edits are whole-inspection last-writer-wins at field granularity with version precondition; no merge UI in v1 (conflict surface is a dialog, see §7).
  • Photos: up to 40 per inspection, compressed for upload (configurable max edge / JPEG quality), originals retained locally until purge.
  • Signature: one inspector signature offline; countersign (if any) is online-only in v1.
  • No offline export/email of reports; report PDF generation stays server-side.

5. Local data model (SQLite + blob store)

Tables (illustrative):

  • inspections(id PK, server_version, state, assigned_to, schema_id, schema_version, downloaded_at, accepted_at, purge_after, dirty)
  • answers(inspection_id, field_id, value_text, value_num, value_bool, updated_at, updated_by, local_rev, sync_state) — PK (inspection_id, field_id)
  • attachments(id PK, inspection_id, kind /*photo|signature*/, local_path, sha256, bytes, mime, captured_at, server_id NULL, upload_state, retries)
  • outbox(id PK, inspection_id, op /*upsert_answers|upload_attachment|submit|ack_purge*/, payload_json, created_at, next_attempt_at, attempts, state)
  • audit_events(id PK, inspection_id, at, actor, device_id, event_type /*edit|photo_add|sign|sync_ok|sync_fail|submit|purge*/, field_id NULL, old_hash, new_hash, prev_event_hash, event_hash) — hash chain per inspection
  • schema_cache(schema_id, version, form_json, fetched_at)

Blobs in files/attachments/<sha256> (content-addressed). DB stores only hashes + paths. All writes go through a single repository layer so purge and audit cannot be bypassed.

local_rev is a per-device monotonic counter for idempotent outbox identity; the server still owns server_version.

6. Sync protocol

Keep REST; extend, don't replace.

  1. Pull GET /inspections/{id}?since_version=v → record + version + state. Also GET /assignments for queue refresh (online only).
  2. Push POST /inspections/{id}/changes with:
    • base_version (precondition),
    • client_change_id (UUID, idempotency key),
    • field upserts (only dirty fields) + client_rev,
    • optional op=submit. Response: 200 new server_version, or 409 with server field snapshot for conflict handling, or 422 schema/validation error (do not retry blindly — surface to user).
  3. Attachments POST /inspections/{id}/attachments (multipart or resumable upload session for photos; chunk size ~1–2 MB). Server returns server_id. Upload is content-hash idempotent. Signature uploads first and is required before submit succeeds.
  4. Purge ack POST /inspections/{id}/purge-ack with device id, purge time, list of deleted hashes.
  5. Ordering per inspection: answer upserts → attachment uploads → submit → (later) purge-ack. Across inspections, outbox is parallelizable but bounded (2 workers) to protect radio/battery.
  6. Idempotency: every push carries client_change_id; server stores it and returns the original result on replay (covers timeout-after-commit).
  7. Clock skew: server timestamps are authoritative for accepted_at / purge_after; device uses them verbatim.

7. Conflict policy

Product decision: no collaborative editor. Policy:

  • Every push requires base_version == server_version, else 409.
  • On 409, app compares dirty local fields vs server snapshot:
    • If disjoint field sets → auto-rebase: retry push with new base_version (still recorded in audit).
    • If same field edited → local field loses (server wins) for supervisor-originated changes; show a non-blocking toast listing overwritten fields, and keep the local discarded value in audit_events (not as an editable answer).
    • If local has submit pending and server moved past submitted/accepted → drop local submit, adopt server state.
  • Photo/signature blobs never conflict (append-only by hash); duplicates deduped server-side.
  • Rationale: supervisors are the reviewers; their online corrections are authoritative. Field-level LWW avoids silent data loss better than whole-record LWW.

8. Attachments

  • Cap 40 photos/inspection (enforced locally and server-side). Show remaining count.
  • Local pipeline: capture → downscale preview → store original → enqueue upload of transfer encoding (config: max edge 2048, JPEG q≈80) plus EXIF time. Strip precise GPS by default (policy flag).
  • Resumable uploads; cancel/retry UI; upload only on Wi-Fi toggle (default on for company-managed devices).
  • Signature stored as PNG/SVG + device-local cert binding (see §9). Hash of signature payload written to audit chain before first upload attempt.

9. Security

  • Auth tokens in platform keystore/Keychain; biometric/passcode device unlock required before first offline edit of the day (gate for offline use of cached credentials).
  • App-private storage; no customer data on shared storage. Screen capture discouraged via OS flags where available (not compliance-critical).
  • All sync traffic TLS 1.2+; certificate pinning optional for pilot (decide via D1).
  • Field-level values never logged; logs carry ids/hashes only.
  • Remote wipe / selective purge command path (MDM or server "revoke inspection" push) — MDM wipe covers lost devices; in-app purge covers retention.
  • Signature integrity: hash of signature image + inspection id + schema version chained into audit; server verifies hash on submit.

10. Migration / backfill

  • Server: add client_change_id idempotency store, attachment upload sessions, purge-ack endpoint, accepted_at on inspections. Additive, versioned (Accept: application/vnd.insp.v2+json).
  • Device: migrate answers table from cache semantics to durable outbox-backed store (local_rev, sync_state columns; backfill local_rev=0). Migration is transactional; on failure app falls back to read-only cache mode and forces resync rather than risking corruption.
  • Existing cached inspections are re-downloaded once (treat as not local-ready until re-fetched) — simplest correct backfill.
  • Audit chain starts at migration time; a server-side audit_created root event anchors the chain per inspection.

11. Observability

  • Client metrics (aggregated, no PII): outbox depth/age, sync success/409/422 rates, attachment upload retries/time-to-drain, purge latency after accepted_at, crash-free sessions on pilot builds.
  • Server metrics: 409 rate by field, idempotent replays, purge-ack lag, attachment integrity mismatches.
  • Per-inspection audit chain export endpoint for regulators (server-side; device contributes events via sync).
  • Alerting: purge-ack lag > 20h in pilot → page on-call (compliance clock).

12. Staged rollout

Stage Cohort Gate to next
S0 Internal dogfood (2–3 devices, synthetic buildings) 2 full offline-to-accept-to-purge cycles per device; zero silent data loss
S1 Pilot: 5–15 inspectors, one region, real jobs ≥ 95% sessions complete offline path without support ticket; purge-ack lag p95 < 12h; 409 auto-rebase success ≥ 90% of conflicts
S2 Expand regions + supervisor conflict tooling polish S1 metrics hold for 2 weeks of real volume
S3 Broad rollout No open Sev-1/2 from S2

Feature flag offline_mode (server-driven) plus minimum app version flag. Kill switch flips devices to read-only cache behavior on next connect.

13. Rollback

  • Flag off: devices stop accepting offline edits; pending outboxes still drain (never discard). New edits require connectivity.
  • App release rollback: schema migrations written backward-compatible within one minor (additive columns only for 2 releases) so store rollback doesn't brick local DB.
  • Server rollback: v2 routes are additive; v1 clients unaffected. Idempotency store retained.
  • Data rollback: purge is destructive by design — not reversible; rollback of the feature does not resurrect purged device data (server remains system of record).
  • Declare explicitly: accept loss of in-flight offline edits only via user-mediated "abandon changes" — no automatic rollback path drops outbox.

14. Testing

  • Unit: outbox state machine, conflict rebase/field-diff, audit hash chain, purge scheduler.
  • Integration: REST sync against staging with fault injection (timeouts after commit, 409 storms, chunked upload interrupt).
  • Device lab: airplane-mode runs for full inspection + 40 photos + signature; intermittent network (monkey/chaos profile); force-quit mid-upload; clock skew.
  • Compliance tests: simulated accepted → verify purge within 24h including device-off window at purge time; audit chain verification job.
  • Pilot exit checklist = S1 gate metrics above.

15. Milestone sequence (no dates, no staffing)

M1 — Offline capture core (depends: schema cache, device storage migration)

  • Durable answers + local attachments + outbox; no network required after download.
  • AC: airplane-mode inspection completion with 40 photos + signature intact after force-quit.

M2 — Sync engine (depends: M1, server idempotent change API, attachment upload API)

  • Ordered drain, retries, 409 auto-rebase, field-conflict surfacing.
  • AC: fault-injection suite green; replaying client_change_id yields single commit.

M3 — Submit + supervisor convergence (depends: M2)

  • Offline submit queue; server 409 policy as in §7; audit chain upload.
  • AC: concurrent supervisor edit vs offline submit converges deterministically; audit chain verifies.

M4 — Retention & purge (depends: M3, accepted_at + purge-ack API)

  • Purge scheduling, receipts, compliance metric.
  • AC: purge-ack p95 < 12h in S0/S1; regulator export job runs clean.

M5 — Pilot hardened (depends: M1–M4, feature flag, observability)

  • Kill switch, Wi-Fi-only toggle, support tooling (per-device sync dump), S0 exit.
  • AC: S0 checklist passed; docs for field support.

M6 — Pilot & evaluate (depends: M5)

  • S1 cohort run; go/no-go for S2.
  • AC: S1 gate metrics met or explicit product sign-off to iterate.

Dependencies: M1→M2→M3→M4 is the critical path; M5 can overlap late M4 (flags/observability); M6 is strictly gated.

16. Decisions required before implementation (with recommendations)

ID Decision Recommendation Why
D1 TLS pinning on/off Off for pilot, measure; decide before S2 Pinning complicates enterprise proxy debugging; pilot risk is low on managed networks
D2 Photo transfer encoding vs originals Upload compressed derivative + hashes of original; keep original until purge Bandwidth in basements/street 4G; regulators accept hash-anchored originals
D3 GPS in EXIF Strip by default; site id is the location record Avoid precise home location retention; simpler purge surface
D4 Conflict: who wins on same-field edit Server (supervisor) wins; local discarded value kept in audit only Matches "not a collaborative editor"; reviewers are authoritative
D5 Offline auth lifetime Require daily unlock (biometric/passcode) to use cached creds; session ≤ 7 days device-bound Limits lost-device exposure while keeping full-shift offline
D6 Purge receipt contents Store id, times, hash list only — no field values Satisfies A4 with minimal residual risk; easy to extend if auditors demand more
D7 Attachment upload radio policy Wi-Fi preferred, cellular allowed after 30 min backlog Pilot devices are cellular-enabled; don't strand evidence
D8 Schema evolution while offline Pin schema_version at download; edits with old schema still accepted if field ids stable; force re-download if schema breaking: true Avoids mid-form surprise; server remains flexible
D9 Signature acceptance offline Capture full signature packet offline; mark submitted only after signature hash reaches server Signature is the legal artifact; don't let submit succeed without it
D10 Kill-switch behavior Read-only cache mode, outbox drains, no new offline edits Never destroy user work when disabling the feature

17. Open questions (explicitly deferred)

  • Countersignature workflow and whether it needs offline support (assume no for pilot).
  • Cross-inspection batching for sites with many units.
  • Long-term device audit retention if A4 is later rejected by compliance.
